# Architect rulings: S4-02c review F1 Q1â€“Q3 and R1 (2026-10-14)

Inputs: `.agent/S4-02c-review.md` F1 and registry closeout notes, `.agent/S4-02c-review-packet.md`, `.agent/S4-02c-walkthrough-evidence.md` run 1, `tasks/S4-02c-evidence-consequence-work.md` R1; `D-2026-10-09-s2-simulated-publication-clock`, the S3 dispatch decisions, `D-2026-10-14-s402-post-review-follow-ons`, and `D-2026-10-14-s5-selected-window-overview`. This is a ruling for subsequent work, not a change to S4-02c's reviewed contract.

## Q1 â€” sequence and source time

**Rule:** An admission-allocated gateway sequence is transport/admission order, not proof of source-time order. For a registered same-source, same-epoch stream whose source-time/interval semantics and clock quality are attested and valid, S3 must place readings by source time. A later gateway sequence with an earlier source time is a late arrival, not `SEQUENCE_ORDER_INVERTED` by itself. Preserve duplicate, overlap, conflicting epoch, untrusted clock, and impossible-time refusals; where the source-time basis cannot be established, report a named series limit rather than silently sort and claim coherence. This does not bless arbitrary backdating. The Lab-delayed publication clock supplies a deterministic case: the withheld report has no sequence before release, so admission numbering naturally runs backward against source time.

**Owner and timing:** S3 cohort/assessment (`dispatch-3.x`) plus S2 gateway/publication-profile proof, in `I-late-reading-series-and-money-limit`, **before S5-01's money read and before a real-Site pilot**. Changing the reviewed cohort rule requires a new model version, reassessment/reconciliation of affected current windows and preservation of old `dispatch-3.1` revisions and exact links as history. The implementer must prove both on-time-sequenced late upload and admission-sequenced delayed admission through accepted public evidence. A real gateway without validated source-time/clock semantics stays limited.

## Q2 â€” Finding limit and money

**Rule:** A revised Finding must carry the selected assessment's material `GENERATOR_SERIES_AMBIGUOUS`/`SEQUENCE_ORDER_INVERTED` limit when it removes or truncates the prior candidate episode. Its end reason must identify the series refusal; `OPEN_BOUND` alone is false for the run-1 truncation. Preserve the supported `[06:05, 07:00)` portion if its exact evidence remains coherent, but disclose that the later portion is unassessed, not disproved or repaired. S4 owns FindingRevision propagation and its read/UI; a changed claim basis appends a revision.

S5's `mini:4#a` money read **withholds the amount** if generator-series ambiguity affects the selected Finding/window, even if a narrower consequence is `CURRENT PRICED`. Show an explicit no-current-amount state with the assessment/Finding limit and exact links. This is a fail-closed rule for the demonstrated silent understatement, not a claim that every `INDETERMINATE` window has no valid candidate time. Once S3 resolves and reassesses that series under a reviewed rule, S5 may show a same-basis current amount for the proven Candidate span, with any remaining material limits visible. S4 consequence currentness remains a statement about the cited FindingRevision's technical/price basis; S5 eligibility is a stricter presentation gate. Do not rewrite historical $7.5625 or $15.8125 consequences.

**Owner and timing:** S4 Finding projection and S5 `I-site-overview-read`/UI consume the S3 decision in `I-late-reading-series-and-money-limit`; **before S5-01**. The real-Site pilot also needs the source-time/clock proof. A regression must replay run 1 and the source-ordered variant and assert the assessment status, Finding limitations/end reason, consequence lineage and S5 amount eligibility; the 7.5625 USD current consequence must never become an unqualified current Overview amount.

## Q3 â€” placement and gates

**Rule:** F1 gets its own sized node, `I-late-reading-series-and-money-limit` (**M**), because it changes reviewed S3 assessment semantics, S4 claim semantics and S5 money eligibility. It is a hard prerequisite of `I-site-overview-read`, hence S5-01, with `I-S2-02`, `I-S3-01`, `I-S4-01`, `I-S4-02b` as inputs. The node's acceptance target records both late-numbering variants and the no-silent-money result. It also gates the first real-Site pilot. The owner's â€œanalysis input data for simulated and real Sitesâ€ backlog item remains the broader W1-exit contract for per-analytic required inputs, cadence and coverage; it should include source-time/clock attestation at that review, but does not substitute for this narrower S5 defect gate. R1 concerns operating the already defined jobs and is separate from semantic correctness.

## R1 â€” run the S3 and S4 workers

**Rule:** Create `I-analytic-finding-worker-schedule` (**S**), requiring S3, S4 and S4-02c's consequence-work node, and gate `I-site-overview-read` on it. Install or explicitly configure an environment-owned bounded schedule for `process-jobs` then `match-findings` across active tenant/Sites; report backlog, last success/failure, retry and stopped-worker conditions. Keep S5 `PENDING`/`STALE_PROJECTION` while upstream work is outstanding. A late accepted reading must reach a revised Finding and consequence without the manual Leg B commands. The S4-02c one-shot consequence runner and its documented external timer do not schedule S3/S4.

**Owner and timing:** S3/S4 worker operation, with S5 currentness integration; **before S5-01**, and therefore before W1 exit or a real-Site pilot that claims automatic late-evidence recovery. The Planner should refine deployment ownership and cadence in a small follow-on packet. No new scheduler mechanism is mandated if an existing reliable environment timer can meet the acceptance target.

## Acceptance and owner decision

S4-02c remains **accepted with carries**. Its transactional enqueue, scoped drain, one-shot run plan and two-leg authored demonstration satisfy its packet. F1 is upstream S3/S4 semantics and a new S5 eligibility requirement; R1 is unscheduled upstream operations. The reviewer explicitly did not make either an S4-02c acceptance gap. Close `I-evidence-acceptance-consequence-work` on merge after the review's documentation carries; leave the two new nodes open.

**Owner input:** No additional product choice is needed to make these safety and lineage rulings. The owner should see the no-amount state and limited Finding at the existing S5-01 visual checkpoint, and decide any broader analysis-input policy at the W1-exit review. If the owner wants partial, explicitly limited money from an indeterminate generator series, that would be a new product/risk decision with a separate reviewed contract; it is not implied by the current S5 eligibility rule.

## Draft DECISIONS entry

Decision: `D-2026-10-14-s402c-late-sequence-and-s5-money`. The Architect rules that gateway admission sequence does not establish source-time order for a validated store-and-forward source. S3 must version a source-time-aware cohort rule with explicit clock/overlap/epoch limits and reassess affected windows without rewriting history. S4 must carry a material series refusal into the revised Finding and state why the episode ends. Until a reviewed reassessment resolves generator-series ambiguity, S5 withholds an amount for that affected selected Finding/window despite a `CURRENT PRICED` consequence, and shows the limitation and exact lineage. `I-late-reading-series-and-money-limit` and `I-analytic-finding-worker-schedule` are separate prerequisites of `I-site-overview-read` before S5-01; both also gate real-Site readiness. The latter schedules S3/S4 workers and makes backlog/failure visible so late evidence reaches the revised Finding without manual commands. The broader simulated/real analysis-input backlog remains for W1 exit. S4-02c acceptance is unchanged; its reviewed consequence work may close on merge. The S5 owner visual checkpoint remains required.

## Plain-language owner summary

The gateway numbers a delayed reading when it arrives. Today that can make the analysis stop early and show **$7.5625 as current** where the same readings numbered in their source order yield **$15.8125**, without saying the series was cut short. I have gated the S5 money card until the reading order is handled under a reviewed rule and the Finding explains any remaining limit. I also added a small scheduling gate so delayed evidence reaches the revised analysis and Finding automatically. This does not reopen S4-02c's acceptance.

## Question 1: the sequence-order deviation

**Is the change fixture forcing?** No, in the sense N5 guards against. N5 forbids changing the fixture to make
Leg A's precondition hold. Leg A and N5 pass identically under both numberings, and I reproduced both. The change
affects only the S3/S4 re-evaluation in Leg B, which is upstream reviewed code, not this slice. The packet's phase 3
row says only "one mean withheld until a later decided time", so it fixes no numbering. Run 1 is recorded
transparently. Under SOURCE_ORDER, the variant models a reading the gateway sequenced on time whose upload arrived
late. That is a legitimate path, since sealed batches may upload independently (S2-02). I accept it as authoring.

**But the stated rationale is wrong for this repository, and the real-path consequence is understated (F1).**

## Findings

### F1 (Medium, residual risk and Architect question; not an S4-02c acceptance gap): this project's own late-reading path produces run 1, and run 1 yields current S5-eligible money that is understated without saying so

Files:
- `backend/conversions/s4-power-late-demo-cases-v1.json:11` (`sequence_rule`);
- `.agent/S4-02c-walkthrough-evidence.md` "Run 1";
- packet "Residual risk: New (from run 1)";
- `.ai/CODE_STATE.md` ("late-delivery sequence numbering on real gateways unproved").

The fixture says "a store-and-forward gateway numbers readings when it takes them", so the late mean fills its gap.
That holds only when the gateway *takes* the reading on time and the upload is late. In this repository, the
gateway allocates the next sequence at admission (`gateway/assetops_gateway/service.py:9-11`: "read/increment
sequence" inside the admission transaction). Under `D-2026-10-09-s2-simulated-publication-clock`, a Lab-delayed
reading "has no V3 envelope or sequence" until it is released. So the Lab's simulated late delivery, and any device
reading that reaches the gateway late, takes a sequence *after* later readings: exactly run 1's numbering. The
packet calls this "not proved here". It is determinable from the code, and it is the default late path for S2-S7
simulated input.

What run 1 actually does (my reproduction, then dropped):
- S3 marks `SEQUENCE_ORDER_INVERTED: generator_power 07:05..08:00`. The **window** assessment is `INDETERMINATE`
  with `GENERATOR_SERIES_AMBIGUOUS`.
- The revised **FindingRevision** r2 nonetheless stays `SUPPORTED`/`CANDIDATE_AVOIDABLE`, and its episode shrinks
  to `[06:05, 07:00)`:
  - candidate minutes fall from 115 to 55, and indeterminate minutes stay 0;
  - its only limitation is `OPEN_BOUND: no OFF evidence after the interval`. That misattributes the end of the
    interval, which ends because of the series refusal, not because of missing OFF evidence.
- Its consequence drains to **CURRENT PRICED, 7.5625 USD over 11 means**, and the money predicate the tool uses
  (CURRENT, PRICED, amount present) makes it **S5-eligible**. That is less than half of the 15.8125 USD the same
  physical data gives when numbered in source order. Neither the Finding nor the consequence names the inversion.

So the answer to "can a real late reading never yield current money via this path?" is: **it does yield current
money, but understated, and the truncation is silent at the Finding and consequence level.** The packet has the
direction of the risk the wrong way round. The risk is not that money never arrives. It is that money which is too
low arrives as current.

Effect on acceptance:
- **S4-02c:** none. Item 3/5 and Leg A behave identically. The consequence correctly derives from r2's accepted
  inputs, and the S3 cohort rule and the Finding episode limitation are S3-01/S4-01 reviewed behaviour.
- **S5-01 and real-Site readiness:** affected. S5-01's money read (`mini:4#a`) would show this figure as current.

Fix on the branch (Low, documentation): scope the `sequence_rule` text and the evidence or packet residual-risk
wording to "a reading sequenced on time and uploaded late". State that admission-time sequencing (Lab-delayed or
device-late readings) gives run 1, with the measured result above. Do not change the fixture again.

Architect questions (batch them, per the owner's routing):
- **Q1.** Should S3's cohort rule (`backend/assetops_backend/analytics/cohort.py` `series_limits`, docstring lines
  17-20) treat a gateway-admission-sequenced late reading as an inversion? The technical design says "monotonic
  arrival is not required" (technical design, V3 identity paragraph) and that "late or out-of-order evidence
  recomputes the affected and neighboring continuity window" (Assess step 4). Today the rule refuses the whole
  later span.
- **Q2.** When the window is `INDETERMINATE` with `GENERATOR_SERIES_AMBIGUOUS` over part of a Finding's former
  episode, must the revised FindingRevision carry that limitation (rather than only `OPEN_BOUND`)? And must the S5
  money predicate exclude, or label, a consequence whose FindingRevision's window is INDETERMINATE?
- **Q3.** Does this belong with `R1` and the owner's analysis-input backlog item ("analysis input data for
  simulated and real Sites"), or as its own node ahead of S5-01?

### F2 (Low, tool regression in strictness): the S4-02b `recompute` no longer fails when a work item fails

`tools/s402b_fuel_cost_walkthrough.py:169-182`. Before M1, `tree_py(..., check=True)` raised on the runner's exit 1
(any item failed). Now exit 1 is tolerated, failed items are printed, and the function returns normally, so the tool
exits 0. The new `.mjs` claims (`tools/s402b-fuel-cost-evidence.mjs:254-255`) check that exit status and would
therefore pass with failed items. The later API claims would probably still catch a bad amount, but the
"recompute succeeded" claim no longer means what it says. In the same loop, if 50 runs exhaust with backlog left,
the tool also exits silently.

Fix: after printing, `raise SystemExit(1)` when `failed` is non-empty or the loop ended with backlog > 0.

### F3 (Info, housekeeping): earlier-phase scratch databases remain on the shared server

`assetops_s402c_mut_test`, `assetops_s402c_p1_test`, `assetops_s402c2_main_test`, `assetops_s402c2_mut_test`,
`assetops_s402c2_full_test`. None is phase 3's: the phase 3 `s402c3*` databases were dropped, as were all of mine.
They are worth dropping before merge.

  spans, and it also requires the reason to be anything other than `CANDIDATE_SPAN_UNVERIFIED`

# S4-02d â€” analytic and Finding worker schedule

Status: done (merged to main 2026-10-14; fresh slice review `.agent/S4-02d-review.md` ACCEPT with carries R-1..R-3 Low (`caa652ab`); phases 1-2 reviewed ACCEPT with carries; walkthrough 23/23 tick-only; carries in `.ai/MILESTONE_REVIEW_BACKLOG.md` "S4-02d carries"; activated 2026-10-14 from main `0d137768`; next-work `.agent/next-work-2026-10-14m.json` select with 8.28% notice over `I-action-store`; storage ratified `D-2026-10-14-s402d-schedule-storage-and-install-gate`)

## Outcome and authority

An environment-owned, bounded tick runs S3 `process-jobs`, conditionally runs S4 `match-findings` for selected tenant/Sites with a new matcher fence or version, then runs the existing S4-02c consequence drain. A late accepted reading reaches a revised FindingRevision and consequence without an operator invoking the two Leg B commands. Operators can see per-Site backlog, last success/failure, retry and stopped-worker conditions. S5-01 cannot mark a selected window CURRENT while relevant upstream work or an unobserved/stopped worker could leave it behind.

Authority: `.agent/architect-S4-02c-answers.md` R1, `D-2026-10-14-s402c-late-sequence-and-s5-money`, `D-2026-10-14-s5-selected-window-overview`, and `D-2026-10-14-s402d-schedule-storage-and-install-gate` (`.agent/architect-S4-02d-answers.md`). This packet owns registry node `I-analytic-finding-worker-schedule` (S). The separate F1 node owns run-1 sequence numbering and money limitation semantics; this packet does not change S3 cohort rules, Finding limits or price eligibility.

Built anchors: `.ai/CODE_STATE.md` S3-01/S4-01/S4-02c; `backend/assetops_backend/analytics/assess_dispatch.py` (`process-jobs`, `match-findings --tenant-id --site-id`); `analytics/assessment_worker.py` (reconcile, leased jobs and retry); `backend/assetops_backend/recompute_consequences.py` and `financials/recompute.py` (Site claim, attempt-derived retry); `backend/README-db.md` (one-minute per-Site consequence-timer runbook); `tools/s402c_late_power_walkthrough.py` (Leg A/B proof). The current `process-jobs` CLI reconciles all assessed Sites and drains up to 1000 globally leased jobs; `match-findings` is a one-shot Site matcher; the consequence CLI serves only the configured development tenant and one named Site. Do not claim those CLIs already cover all active production Sites.

## Schedule, state and S5 contract

1. **Owned Site inventory and cadence.** One timer per environment invokes a server-side tick, initially every minute. An active Site is a persisted, tenant-scoped imported Site with registered evidence or explicit schedule enrolment, whose lifecycle is neither `DECOMMISSIONED` nor `ARCHIVED`; `PLANNED` qualifies (including KOBO-001). A Site with live S3 or pending consequence work remains selected until that work is resolved, even if its enrolment changes. Report excluded Sites that still have work; never silently discard it. Enumerate by stable IDs from the persisted Site registry on every tick; â€œnewly activatedâ€ in proof means newly imported/enrolled, not a lifecycle transition. Bound Sites, Site-scoped S3 leases, and consequence items per tick with a durable fair cursor. Extend the assessment store's currently global `lease_job` to filter by Site inside the existing assessment writer; its global FIFO cannot provide Site fairness. Run `reconcile_all()` each tick and reconcile every selected Site, including never-assessed Sites; measure its global unbounded cost, without silently omitting it. For each selected Site run S3, then run S4 only when there is no `finding_match_pass` for the current `MATCHER_VERSION`, or the Site's matcher fence (`_fence`: selection seq, job count/marker, revision count) differs from the fence stored in `schedule_site_head` at the last completed pass. Otherwise record `S4 skipped: unchanged fence` in that mutable head and append no pass row. Due, leased or FAILED S3 work does not by itself suppress a match when the fence moves. The matcher's CURRENT-window rule leaves those windows' episodes unchanged; unrelated current windows can advance after a new selection moves the fence. Drain consequence work after Finding commits. A bounded tick may leave work for later ticks.
2. **Concurrency and recovery.** Use database claims for the environment tick and Site stage sequence across hosts; local timer overlap prevention is insufficient. Never overlap S3 leasing/processing and S4 matching on the same Site, including a second host. The matcher fence includes Site recompute-job statuses; report `FindingFenceMoved` as retryable typed S4 `FENCE_MOVED`, not a generic success. Preserve S3's five-minute lease and retry and S4-02c's Site claim/append idempotency. An expired/crashed claim resumes without duplicate output. Bound tick and Site wall time with server-side `statement_timeout` and `lock_timeout`, plus a killable child process per stage for a Python hang. The Site S3 stage bound must be shorter than the five-minute lease; after a killed job, expose its lease expiry and retry, never assert immediate completion. Both DB stall and nonreturning-stage injections must yield typed `TIMEOUT` and retryable work. Give schedule-level discovery/stage failures retry/backoff without advancing success; do not add an independent retry to S3 or consequence rows. A terminal FAILED S3 job stays live and will not be re-enqueued by `reconcile`; expose its ID and `last_error`, alarm, and provide `assess_dispatch requeue --tenant-id --site-id --start --end` through the existing `AssessmentStore.enqueue` path for an operator to re-enqueue that default window after fixing the cause. Document the command and confirm the new DEFAULT selection supersedes the old failure; `assess` creates an EXPLICIT run and is not recovery.
3. **Durable state and health.** Migration `0013` adds `schedule_tick_head` (mutable environment owner, last tick start/success/failure, heartbeat, retry due and claim age), `schedule_site_head` (mutable per-Site claim/stage, selected/processed/deferred counts, last failure and last completed matcher fence), `schedule_cursor` (mutable fair position), and `schedule_enrolment` (mutable explicit Site enrolment). Use DB claims or equivalent expiring ownership in these records; do not add unbounded tick rows by default. The ratified eighth PostgreSQL writer, `backend/assetops_backend/scheduling/adapters/postgres_schedule_store.py`, is the sole runtime writer of these four tables. Amend `tools/checks/configuration-persistence.ps1` rule 8 for this exact adapter path and these tables only: add writer allowlist and non-vacuity checks for an observed SQL write and driver import; allow `INSERT` and name the allowed mutable schedule-state `UPDATE` columns in the guard. Preserve reverse ownership: no other runtime module writes these tables, and this adapter writes no other product table. Test rejection of unlisted columns/tables. No wildcard SQL or driver exception, `DELETE`, `TRUNCATE`, runtime DDL, or weakening of the seven existing writers is allowed. Register a schedule domain composition in adapter-isolation checks if it imports this adapter; callers receive its port. Migration DDL is separate from runtime writing; any proposed trigger needs separate review. Claims and deadlines use DB time. Keep durable heads bounded; if attempt history is added, set a retention policy while preserving the latest failure and aggregate counts. Derive S3 backlog from live jobs, S4 backlog from existing `PostgresFindingStore.match_state` / `finding_match_pass` versus `analytic_run_selection.seq`, and consequence backlog from `ConsequenceStore.pending_work` with attempt count/age. Also count pre-0012 latest consequence heads whose read says `STALE` plus `NO_WORK_RECORDED` as distinct `STALE_NO_WORK` entries with Finding/revision IDs. This slice reports those discrepancies and blocks CURRENT; it does not backfill them. A read-only operator report gives each Site and aggregate `RUNNING`, `BACKLOG`, `RETRY_DUE`, `FAILED`, `STOPPED` or healthy idle. Only a completed zero-work tick advances heartbeat; claimed/skipped, failed and timed-out ticks do not. Repeated Site-claim skips, stale in-flight claims, old failures and growing uncapped consequence attempts trigger alarms and documented recovery.
   The rule 8 update scope is environment tick claim/lease, start/success/failure, heartbeat and retry due in `schedule_tick_head`; Site claim/lease, stage, selected/processed/deferred counts, last failure and completed matcher fence/skip state in `schedule_site_head`; fair position in `schedule_cursor`; and explicit enrolment state in `schedule_enrolment`. Name actual columns under those categories in the guard. S3 jobs and selections, Findings, accepted evidence, Foundation Sites, financial assumptions, and consequence work/revisions/attempts stay under their existing writers.
4. **S5-01 consumption.** Add a backend Python scoped read/port taking `(tenant_id, site_id, [window_start, window_end))`. Return the S3 `UNASSESSED|PENDING|CURRENT|STALE_PROJECTION|FAILED` read status and limits, accepted input/source basis, relevant live S3 job IDs/status/errors, selected `analytic_run_selection.seq` and run/revision IDs, `match_state` pass ID/matched-through seq and matched Finding revision IDs, consequence head/work IDs, status/attempts/age (including `STALE_NO_WORK`), schedule state/heartbeat, DB `clock_timestamp()`, and a derived upstream eligibility status with stage/reason. The accepted-source watermark is the selected S3 input basis plus its `analytic_run_selection.seq`; do not use disposition timestamps or a later heartbeat as a commit-order fence (`D-2026-10-11-s3-phase-2-selection-and-read`). Acceptance transactionally creates/widens a job. For each window and its six-hour continuity bracket, CURRENT eligibility requires no intersecting live S3 job, current S3 basis, `match_state.matched = true`, no relevant outstanding or stale-without-work consequence, and a healthy observed schedule; a stopped/unobserved schedule blocks it even with empty queues. Use DB time for heartbeat/STOPPED threshold and avoid app-clock assumptions. First build with work is `PENDING`; an old result with advanced basis/work is `STALE_PROJECTION`; failed with no usable prior result is `FAILED`, preserving typed stage/error. The existing S3 read status, matcher pass and pending-work derivations are authority; S5-01 consumes this seam and applies its separate F1 money rule and same-basis `CURRENT/PRICED` test. Historical IDs remain readable.

**Per-environment ownership and timer transition (phase 2).** Installation or enablement of a persistent schedule on any owner-visible host, including the demo host, is gated on `I-late-reading-series-and-money-limit` landing. Private development and isolated walkthrough ticks may run before then. On installing this tick, retire the S4-02c one-timer-per-Site consequence schedule in `backend/README-db.md`; keep its CLI for one-off operator recovery. A simultaneous legacy timer would hold the Site claim and produce false skip alarms. Document exact identity, credential, cadence, bounds, stop threshold and restart commands in that runbook.

| Environment | Tick owner and identity | Cadence / gate |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Local development | Developer's local scheduler; local database credential | Optional one-minute tick on a private DB; remove the old per-Site timer when enabled. |
| Demo host | Deployment operator; dedicated schedule credential scoped to that database | One minute only after `I-late-reading-series-and-money-limit` lands; before then do not install. |
| Isolated walkthrough database | Walkthrough runner with isolated DB credential | Explicit tick invocations for repeatable proof; no persistent timer. |
| Real-Site pilot | Pilot deployment operator and separately provisioned schedule credential | Not installed until `real_pilot_requires` and other pilot gates close; then one environment timer at reviewed cadence. |

## Phases and focused proof

Each phase is a reviewable result fitting one Claude Implementer context below approximately 120k tokens; obtain an independent fresh review before proceeding.

| Phase | Reviewable result and focused proof |
| --- | --- |
| 1. Bounded orchestration and storage | Migration 0013, new schedule writer and reviewed rule 8 amendment; persisted active-Site enumeration, fair bounded tick, Site-scoped S3 leasing, ordered S3 â†’ conditional S4 â†’ consequence invocation, cross-host claims and timeout/restart behavior. Prove two tenants, newly imported/enrolled `PLANNED` Site, excluded/foreign Site reporting, more Sites/jobs than one tick limit, two concurrent ticks, crash/restart and idempotent replay. Inject DB stall and a nonreturning stage. Prove N idle ticks append zero `finding_match_pass` rows and a new selection appends exactly one pass, while retaining the per-window currentness property: S4 runs but does not change episodes over a due/leased/FAILED S3 window, that window cannot be CURRENT, and another current window's Finding still advances. |
| 2. Health, install and currentness handoff | Durable/derived Site report, last success/failure, retry due and stopped/hung alarm; selected-window S5 seam; installable timer behind the F1 gate and revised `backend/README-db.md` runbook that retires the per-Site consequence timer. Supply DB time to the scheduled consequence runner's `now` input, leaving consequence rows under their existing writer; a one-off operator run uses the same DB-clock default or reports an override. Prove idle heartbeat, failing matcher and typed `FENCE_MOVED`, terminal S3 failure/requeue, repeated claim skips, stopped timer beyond threshold, permanent consequence failure, `STALE_NO_WORK`, and recovery. Assert pending/stale/failed versus eligible CURRENT after reload and skewed application clocks against DB time, including consequence retry timing; never use queue-empty alone. |
| 3. Automatic late-power walkthrough | Extend or reuse `tools/s402c_late_power_walkthrough.py` in an isolated DB. Drive first delivery and all subsequent steps by full schedule ticks; record the exact accepted ID and pre-tick `STALE/TECHNICAL_INPUT_CHANGED` plus queued work. When the withheld GOOD mean is released, the first tick may append Finding r2 before draining r1 work: assert r1's `EVIDENCE_ACCEPTED` attempt is a typed `FINDING_REVISION_SUPERSEDED` no-op, then r2's consequence reaches its new CURRENT result. Cite the S4-02c walkthrough for the separate same-revision `POWER_NOT_IN_ASSESSMENT_INPUTS` Leg A evidence; do not require that transient state from this tick-only proof. Assert the revised FindingRevision cites the late ID and the upstream S5 seam becomes eligible only after work completes. Record real job/run/selection-seq/match-pass/revision/work/attempt/consequence IDs, watermarks, backlog and heartbeat before/after, replay and stopped-worker state. Capture the command log and grep to prove no hand-run `process-jobs`/`match-findings`/`recompute_consequences run` in any Leg B step (W4). Record run-1 S3 `SEQUENCE_ORDER_INVERTED`/`GENERATOR_SERIES_AMBIGUOUS` status and limits where present, and assert only the seam's true upstream state; money eligibility belongs to F1. Use the existing source-ordered variant for a positive CURRENT path. |

Reviewer checks Site inventory and isolation, per-tick limits/fairness, ordering, claim/timeout/retry, restart idempotency, stage-specific errors, heartbeat and backlog truth, currentness under race, and the no-manual-Leg-B artifact. The independent review must race two overlapping ingestion chunks against an active tick and prove no unresolved deadlock, no falsely CURRENT selected window, and eventual reprocessing after a moved basis. The schedule claim does not serialise ingestion; if the proof finds an ingestion transaction or lock-order defect, carry it to the evidence/ingestion owner in a separate scoped packet and keep I4 open until verified. Run focused Postgres worker tests and the isolated walkthrough plus applicable architecture/workflow guards. `USER_REVIEW_REQUIRED` stays false only while the output remains operator/read-contract work; S5-01 retains the owner visual checkpoint.

## Risks and carries

- S3's existing global `reconcile_all()` can be expensive as Sites grow; measure a multi-Site tick and keep bounded scheduling truthful. Do not omit reconcile to meet a timer budget.
- The matcher fence aggregates all Site recompute-job statuses; its `string_agg` can grow without bound. Track it alongside global reconcile cost. Serialise S3 and S4 for a Site even across hosts.
- S4 matching currently scans a Site's selected reads. Large Sites may require a reviewed bounded cursor or dirty-window selection to meet the tick limit; do not mark a partially matched Site caught up.
- S4-02c retries have no terminal cap: a deterministic failed row can append about 90 attempts/day. Keep the growing count and backlog observable and alertable; do not hide it by reporting only due work.
- A hung consequence runner can hold a Site claim while later ticks report skipped. Treat repeated skips/claim age as a stopped-progress alarm, with operator recovery; do not count skipped as success.
- Backdated configuration/model changes can dirty assessed windows without a fresh evidence job. The S3 reconcile and S4 basis comparison must detect them before CURRENT is possible.
- Do not install the schedule on an owner-visible host before `I-late-reading-series-and-money-limit` lands: it would automatically move late evidence to the run-1 too-low consequence on the S4-02b money panel.
- S4-02c I3 remains open until DB time controls heartbeat, claim expiry, retry due, STOPPED detection, the S5 seam and the scheduled consequence runner's `now`; a heartbeat-only fix does not close it. I4 remains open until the independent two-chunk overlapping-ingestion proof passes; use live-job/selection/match comparisons and do not claim the schedule serialises ingestion.

Registry comparison: `queue_baseline_nodes` already contains `I-analytic-finding-worker-schedule`; keep it there and map its `task` to S4-02d. S5-01's `I-site-overview-read` remains gated on this node and the separate F1 node. No baseline node moves.

Out of scope: F1 late-reading sequence semantics (`I-late-reading-series-and-money-limit`), new cohort or fuel model, new owner UI/copy, S5-01 implementation, real gateway source-time attestation, and a general estate scheduler for unrelated domains.

### Implementer carries from fresh re-review

- **N2 â€” timeout propagation.** S3, Finding and consequence stores each construct an engine. Apply `statement_timeout` and `lock_timeout` to each stage child through its connection settings or schedule credential without moving engine construction into the orchestrator or editing another writer. Database access by the orchestrator and S5 seam remains through writer/store modules; rule 8 still rejects new SQL or driver imports outside the allowed writers.
- **N3 â€” deterministic matcher failure.** `FindingFenceMoved` can also mean a cited evidence ID is not accepted in this Site. Distinguish that deterministic defect from a moved fence, or alarm on repeated `FENCE_MOVED` for the same Site so it cannot retry invisibly forever.
- **N4 â€” registered evidence.** Define the active-Site predicate's â€œregistered evidenceâ€ as at least one `accepted_evidence` row, or explicitly include registered gateway/source state. Prove an imported Site with neither is excluded unless enrolled and a Site with a gateway but no evidence follows the chosen definition.
- **N5 â€” failed-job requeue.** `assess_dispatch requeue` must overlap the FAILED job's window for the new DEFAULT selection to supersede that failure; prefer `--job-id` or validate `--start/--end`. Preserve UUID tenant handling, avoid a demo-only restriction, and invoke the scheduled consequence stage by IDs rather than its one-off CLI.
- **N6 â€” tick-only walkthrough.** Grep the full command log for `recompute_consequences run` as well as `process-jobs` and `match-findings`. Set per-tick limits so KOBO-001 completes S3 and S4 on the first post-release tick. If S3 times out, report the actual same-revision or deferred state instead of asserting the r2 path.
# Backend testing guide

How to run the backend suite, when to run all of it, and how to prove a full
run was real. Database setup itself is in [README-db.md](README-db.md).

## When to run what

- **While building a phase:** run the focused files for the area you changed,
  plus adjacent integration tests. When an interface crosses into the host or
  gateway, also run those suites.
- **At the end of an ordinary slice:** one real-Postgres full backend suite,
  on the slice's final revision, with the JUnit zero-skip check below. Cite it
  in the review packet.
- **Reviewers** run the focused files relevant to their findings and cite the
  implementer's same-revision full evidence. A reviewer reruns the full suite
  only for a finding that needs it.
- **One full suite at a time on this machine.** Concurrent full runs were not
  attempted and are not proven safe; a full run is disk-bound, so a second one
  (or another Postgres-heavy agent) slows both and makes timing meaningless.
- OPS-01's baseline and comparison runs are the one documented exception to
  "one full suite per slice".
- `tools/check-all.ps1` is a separate required guard command; it does not run
  pytest, and pytest does not replace it.

## The test database

`ASSETOPS_TEST_DATABASE_URL` names a base database whose name ends in `_test`
(Compose creates `assetops_test`; agents may use a private one such as
`assetops_<task>_test`). Without it every Postgres module is reported as
**skipped**, and such a run is not valid full-suite proof.

The base database is used only as an administrative connection. At
configuration time `tests/conftest.py` calls `pg_harness.route()`, which
rewrites `ASSETOPS_TEST_DATABASE_URL` to a run-owned name before any test module
is imported:

```
<base without _test>_pt<run>_<tag>_test     tag = main (serial), gw0, gw1... (xdist workers), tpl (template)
```

On first use the harness migrates the template to head once, then every
Postgres module's `engine` fixture drops the run's worker database `WITH
(FORCE)` and clones it from the template (`CREATE DATABASE ... TEMPLATE`), and
asserts the clone is at the Alembic head. Each module therefore still starts on
a fresh, fully migrated schema; migration up/downgrade tests still run in place
on that module's clone. `store` fixtures yield and dispose their engine.

Between tests the S1 `store` fixture empties the same tables
`TRUNCATE foundation_revision, source, gateway, asset, site_import, site, tenant
CASCADE` would, but with `DELETE` under `session_replication_role = replica`
(`pg_harness.empty_tables`). TRUNCATE rebuilds every index and on PostgreSQL 16
each rebuild forces a data-file sync, which Docker Desktop makes slow and
erratic (measured 1.4 s quiet, 22-25 s under disk pressure, per test; DELETE
0.14 s). `test_pg_harness.py` proves the DELETE set equals the tables TRUNCATE
CASCADE reports. `ASSETOPS_TEST_CLEANUP=truncate` restores TRUNCATE.

- In parallel (`-n 2`) the xdist controller runs no tests. It makes the run
  id and passes it to each worker; each worker routes itself to
  `..._pt<run>_gw<N>_test` before it imports a test module. The first worker
  that needs the template builds it under an advisory lock and the others reuse
  it. Workers drop only their own database; the controller drops the template
  and anything else the run left (a crashed worker's database) at the end.
  Under `--dist loadfile` every module stays on one worker, so module-scoped
  fixtures behave as serially. With `--no-loadscope-reorder` modules are handed
  out in collection order, and workers move the two long S4 Finding modules
  (`conftest.START_FIRST`) to the front so they start first; serial order is
  unchanged.
- Parametrize IDs must not be random: xdist refuses to run when workers collect
  different node IDs (`Different tests were collected between gw0 and gw1`).
- Nothing is provisioned for `--collect-only`, for a selection without Postgres
  modules, or with the URL unset.
- At the end of the session the run drops its own databases. A killed run's
  databases are swept by the next run with the same base: it drops
  `<base>_pt*` databases only when their run holds no live session (each run
  keeps one lease session whose `application_name` is
  `assetops-test-run:<run>`).
- `ASSETOPS_TEST_TEMPLATE=0` turns routing off and restores the old behaviour
  (drop `public` and migrate in place per module). Use it only if cloning is
  unavailable; it is serial-only and slower.
- Measurement knobs, off by default: `ASSETOPS_TEST_CLONE_STRATEGY`
  (`WAL_LOG` or `FILE_COPY`) and `ASSETOPS_TEST_SYNCHRONOUS_COMMIT` (applied with
  `ALTER DATABASE` to run-owned databases only). Server-wide settings are never
  changed by the tests.

## Commands (PowerShell, from the repository root)

```powershell
$env:ASSETOPS_TEST_DATABASE_URL = "postgresql+psycopg://assetops:assetops-local-dev@127.0.0.1:55432/assetops_test"
$py = ".venv\Scripts\python.exe"

# S1 Foundation and bindings
& $py -m pytest backend/tests/test_s1_foundation_postgres.py backend/tests/test_s1_bindings_postgres.py backend/tests/test_s1_cutover.py backend/tests/test_s1_bindings.py

# S2 ingestion and evidence
& $py -m pytest backend/tests/test_s2_evidence_ledger_postgres.py backend/tests/test_s2_gateway_access_postgres.py backend/tests/test_s2_site_evidence_postgres.py backend/tests/test_s2_registered_basis_postgres.py

# S3 dispatch
& $py -m pytest backend/tests/test_s3_dispatch_assessment_postgres.py backend/tests/test_s3_dispatch_read_postgres.py backend/tests/test_s3_demo_configuration.py

# S4 Finding and money
& $py -m pytest backend/tests/test_s4_finding_store_postgres.py backend/tests/test_s4_finding_read_postgres.py backend/tests/test_s4_finding_evidence_history_postgres.py backend/tests/test_s4_assumptions_postgres.py backend/tests/test_s4_consequence_store_postgres.py backend/tests/test_s4_consequence_recompute_postgres.py backend/tests/test_s4_evidence_consequence_work_postgres.py backend/tests/test_s4_evidence_drain_postgres.py backend/tests/test_s4_power_demo_case_postgres.py backend/tests/test_s4_schedule_tick_postgres.py backend/tests/test_s4_schedule_health_postgres.py

# Complete suite - the normal full command (two workers), with JUnit for the zero-skip proof
& $py -m pytest backend/tests -n 2 --dist loadfile --no-loadscope-reorder -q --durations=30 --junitxml=.pytest-junit.xml

# Complete suite, serial (fallback; also what a serial re-check of one file uses)
& $py -m pytest backend/tests -q --durations=30 --junitxml=.pytest-junit.xml
```

### Before a long full run

Start the keep-awake helper in a separate window (or as a background job). It
asks Windows not to idle-sleep while any pytest process runs, changes no power
settings, and exits after 20 idle minutes. A machine that sleeps mid-run turns
a 40-minute suite into hours (seen in OPS-01: one run reached 34% in 4 h).

```powershell
powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File tools\keep-awake-during-tests.ps1
```

Cap every full run at twice its expected wall time (see Timing below). A run
past the cap is reported as a hang, with the node ID it is stuck on (from
`pg_stat_activity` and the pytest log), not waited on.

### Zero-skip proof (from JUnit, not the summary line)

```powershell
[xml]$j = Get-Content .pytest-junit.xml
$cases = $j.SelectNodes("//testcase")
$skipped = $j.SelectNodes("//testcase[skipped]")
$failed = $j.SelectNodes("//testcase[failure or error]")
"cases=$($cases.Count) skipped=$($skipped.Count) failed=$($failed.Count)"
$skipped | ForEach-Object { "$($_.classname)::$($_.name) - $($_.skipped.message)" }
```

A full run is valid proof only when `skipped` is 0 and `failed` is 0. A skip
whose message names `ASSETOPS_TEST_DATABASE_URL` means the database was unset.

## Timing

Measured on this Windows host (Docker Desktop Postgres 16), serial, full
suite. Run-to-run variance is large (disk sync inside Docker Desktop), so
compare only back-to-back runs in a quiet window.

| Revision | Runs (wall) | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Before OPS-01 (in-place migrate per module, TRUNCATE per test) | 80.3 min, 41.4 min; peak 91 sessions | 2 timing-sensitive tick failures in the 80-min run; a third attempt reached 34% in 4 h while the machine slept |
| Serial template clone + DELETE cleanup (OPS-01 phase 1) | 30.8 min (back-to-back with the 41.4-min baseline); peak 22 sessions | template clone without DELETE cleanup: 41.4 min, one 23-min disk stall in a tick test |
| `-n 2 --dist loadfile` (OPS-01 phase 2, normal command) | 7.7 min, 8.4 min; peak 35 and 32 sessions | 2128 passed, 0 skipped, 0 failed both times; node IDs identical to serial collection |
| `-n 3 --dist loadfile` (OPS-01 phase 2, one run) | 6.5 min; peak 41 sessions | 2128 passed; not repeated, so not the default |

Expected `-n 2` full run: about 8-10 min; cap at 20 min. Expected serial full
run: about 30-45 min; cap at 90 min. Use `-n 2`, never `-n auto`: the suite is
disk-bound inside Docker Desktop, and each worker adds about 10-20 sessions
against `max_connections = 100`.

If a timing-sensitive test (S4 tick hung-stage and claims replay, health skew
and overlap race, drain 30 s deadline, work enqueue-lock poll) fails under
`-n 2`, do not retry it: record the node ID and its duration, rerun that file
serially as a separate invocation, and report both runs' JUnit counts.

These are current implementation authority under project routing; their proposal headers do not record owner sign-off. The [superseded T0xx queue](archive/pre-site-first-2026-10-07/README.md) and its open packets are historical reference with original status intact. ## Mock-gap closure queue

Owner confirmed the catalogue and orphan guard on 2026-10-12 (`D-2026-10-12-mock-gap-closure-mechanism` sections 1â€“2). The non-core ranking and per-screen completion policy wait for W1 exit. These G packets are planning/backfill work and add no scored W1 core paths. G-01 does not gate V-01a's current owner review or merge. The normal single-active-task and owner checkpoints still apply.

| Batch | Packet | Manifest scope | Dependency | Size / status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Shared | [G-01 catalogue and guard](G-01-mock-gap-catalogue-and-guard.md) | Versioned registry, checker, typed missing state and orphan guard | Confirmed mechanism | M; done; owner accepted conditionally 2026-10-13; merged |
| Pilot | [G-02 V-01a gap pilot](G-02-v01a-gap-pilot.md) | `sites`, `foundation`, `readiness`, `sld` | Reviewed G-01 and V-01a merged | M; done; owner accepted conditionally 2026-10-13; merged |
| Backfill | [G-03 Lab/Evidence](G-03-lab-evidence-gap-backfill.md) | `lab`, `evidence`, `evidence-analysis` | Reviewed G-02; merged V-01b UI | M; outline |
| Backfill | [G-04 Dispatch/Finding](G-04-dispatch-finding-gap-backfill.md) | Dispatch, Analysis, Finding family | Reviewed G-02; merged claim UI | M; outline |
| Backfill | [G-05 Money/Overview/Work/Health](G-05-money-overview-work-health-gap-backfill.md) | Remaining W1 screens | Reviewed G-02; merged owner UI | M; outline, split if refinement exceeds M |

The dependency registry carries `I-G-01`â€“`I-G-05` as unscored technical packet nodes. They have no reviewed path to an approved W1 core panel, so `next-work` cannot select them through current core scoring. They are listed separately in its report and activate by `D-2026-10-13-g-gap-packet-activation`: after the active V-01b closes and merges, G-01 takes the next free single-active slot, then G-02 takes the next free slot after reviewed and merged G-01 and merged/accepted V-01a, ahead of an ordinary ranked selection once any checker refusal or owner stop is resolved. G-03â€“G-05 wait for their reviewed merged UI and packet prerequisites; run at the first free slot with no ready active-wave core candidate, or take their remaining backlog to W1-exit review. A â‰¤5% ranking contest still goes to the owner before activating a due G packet; a 5â€“10% notice is recorded. This ordering needs no new owner choice and gives no G node a demo value. The screen ledger's `gap_backfill_task` is a planning pointer, not a delivered gap ID. G-01 introduces the catalogue schema and one real tagged Readiness gateway-firmware missing state; G-02 enrolls the four V-01a screens for complete non-core/open `gap_ids` coverage, then later batches enroll their merged UI.

## Screen-outward planning gate

The [v6.9 screen realization map](../frontend/docs/architecture/v69-screen-realization-map.md), [screen ledger](../frontend/docs/architecture/v69-screen-ledger.json), [model homes](../frontend/docs/architecture/v69-model-homes.json) and [panel contracts](../frontend/docs/architecture/v69-panel-contracts.json) are Architect planning overlays, not accepted screen completion. They account for 110 manifest IDs, 88 model names and 258 visible panels. The owner approved `Real with honest gaps` as the W1 target for 27 screens, including six moved from W4, and their exact core keys/values; later-wave tiers remain pending. The manifest ID and its `visual_reference` key each later comparison; `GAP` in a panel contract means no supported claim yet. Run `python frontend/docs/architecture/check_v69_ledger.py` after ledger changes.

For each `Deferred` screen, the ledger's `promotion_dependencies.to_candidate` lists the design, projection, fixture and task gates before it can enter the executable queue. For every proposed `Real with honest gaps` screen, `promotion_dependencies.to_real` names unresolved panels and remaining work before full mock fidelity can be claimed. A named S task alone does not satisfy that gate. The Planner checks these fields when refining a packet and updates them when a dependency is met or the owner changes a tier.

S1 and S2 are merged; W1 owner input and `A-S3-dispatch` are recorded. **Before S3-01 is activated**, pass PRE-S3-03/startup guards and check the split [V-01 umbrella](V-01-v69-visual-fidelity.md). S3-01 contains the versioned `I-sim-dispatch-cases` fixture as phase 1, followed by typed assessment and UI/cutover phases; its Candidate path stays Indeterminate until the new capability channels are accepted. S3-02 owns the later positive Evidence Analysis claim-limit view. S4-03 owns Finding Evidence/History; S4-04 owns the Site Finding list. S5-01 owns selected-window context/timeline/money; S5-02 integrates the list with separate empty and positive fixtures. S6-01 owns Action detail/activity/change, S6-02 owns plan/evidence/outcome, and S6-03 owns the Site Work list. Resolve only model gaps merited by the next supported claim; broader work, domains and indexes stay in later waves. Unrefined S4-S7 packets remain outlines until their technical gates and owner checkpoints pass. A target tier does not authorize an empty route or illustrative mock value as product data.

S2-02 refinement compared `queue_baseline_nodes`: `I-accepted-evidence-store`, `I-edge-diagnostics-store` and `I-S2-02` remain in S2-02; S2-01b/c retain their nodes. None leaves the baseline. The registry and checker include 19 S1â€“S7 task packets, including S4-04, S5-02 and S6-03. Changed W1 core panel paths await independent graph/mapping review; unchanged reviewed paths keep their citations. Owner target approval is recorded separately.

| --- | --- | --- | --- |

## Non-executable follow-on dependency lane after S7

These are **planning lanes, not task IDs or executable packets**. Review the owner-approved tier and the ledger's exact panel contract before creating a slice. Keep multi-site and multi-window fixtures traceable through normal ingestion. A global comparison needs aligned windows, coverage, currency/price basis and non-overlap rules; Unknown stays visible when those conditions are absent.

## Current-code transition by slice

| Slice | Keep/adapt | Replace or retire at the visible cutover |
| --- | --- | --- |
| S1 | Existing Site shell/Foundation presentation and explicit Kobo fixture | YAML/JSON Site writes for the supported Foundation path become Postgres revisions; old files remain read-only comparison inputs. |
| S2-01a/b/c | Simulated observation transform, mapping and envelope publisher | Kobo Lab uses the registered snapshot; a separate SQLite-backed simulated gateway owns V3 identity, then diagnostics and fault scenarios. Physical protocol proof waits for the first real pilot. |
| S2-02 | Existing ingestion validation/deduplication and Evidence UI | Batch V1 remains readable for migration; authenticated V2 HTTPS receipts and Postgres decisions replace in-process Commit-to-ingest and JSON evidence as product authority. |
| S3-S5 | Existing dispatch, Finding and Site presentation where claim semantics still fit | Hard-coded device keys, JSON assessment authority and independent view recalculation give way to typed accepted-evidence inputs, revisioned claims and derived read models. |
| S6-S7 | Existing Lab control and Site shell where they fit | Persist Action/verification/health claims from public evidence and work records; no Lab truth or static mock value becomes product history. |

3:How to run the backend suite, when to run all of it, and how to prove a full
11:- **At the end of an ordinary slice:** one real-Postgres full backend suite,
15:  implementer's same-revision full evidence. A reviewer reruns the full suite
17:- **One full suite at a time on this machine.** Concurrent full runs were not
18:  attempted and are not proven safe; a full run is disk-bound, so a second one
21:  "one full suite per slice".
23:  pytest, and pytest does not replace it.
30:**skipped**, and such a run is not valid full-suite proof.
45:a fresh, fully migrated schema; migration up/downgrade tests still run in place
63:  Under `--dist loadfile` every module stays on one worker, so module-scoped

110:### Before a long full run
112:Start the keep-awake helper in a separate window (or as a background job). It
113:asks Windows not to idle-sleep while any pytest process runs, changes no power
118:powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File tools\keep-awake-during-tests.ps1
121:Cap every full run at twice its expected wall time (see Timing below). A run
122:past the cap is reported as a hang, with the node ID it is stuck on (from
123:`pg_stat_activity` and the pytest log), not waited on.
128:[xml]$j = Get-Content .pytest-junit.xml
136:A full run is valid proof only when `skipped` is 0 and `failed` is 0. A skip

"""Public report cohort selection for one window (pure; S3-01 phase 2).

A cohort is a set of public report streams (gateway, registered source,
sequence epoch). The rule (dispatch-3 table, "Cohort"):

- A source whose readings in the window come from two or more sequence
  epochs has no coherent default choice: the result is AMBIGUOUS and names each
  epoch as an alternative. This holds whether the epochs' source-time spans
  overlap (two runs) or follow one another (a reprovisioned gateway, or a
  labelled fault fixture placed beside ordinary evidence): central evidence
  carries no attested marker that would tell these apart, so the default never
  blends epochs (phase-2 review F3, `D-2026-10-11-s3-phase-2-selection-and-read`).
  An explicit selection may still name one or more non-overlapping epochs.
- An explicit selection uses exactly the named members; readings of other
  members are excluded, never blended.
- Inside a selected member, each channel (role, asset) must be one series:
  a reading sequenced after another but placed earlier in source time
  (`SEQUENCE_ORDER_INVERTED`) or two readings at one channel-time
  (`REPORTS_OVERLAP`) mark that span unusable. Neither sequence ranges nor
  receipt order is used to split or pick between them.

No epoch name, manifest label or Lab run identifier is consulted.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections import defaultdict
from datetime import datetime

from assetops_backend.analytics.assessment_inputs import (
    AcceptedReading,
    CohortLimit,
    CohortMember,
    CohortSelection,
    Window,
)


def _overlaps(a: tuple[datetime, datetime], b: tuple[datetime, datetime]) -> bool:
    return a[0] <= b[1] and b[0] <= a[1]


def select_cohort(readings: tuple[AcceptedReading, ...], window: Window,
                  explicit: tuple[CohortMember, ...] | None = None) -> CohortSelection:
    """`readings` cover the read range (window plus bracket). Only streams with a reading inside
    the window itself are candidates; a stream seen only in the bracket is not pulled in."""
    present = sorted({r.member for r in readings if window.contains(r.placed_at)})
    if explicit is not None:
        chosen = tuple(sorted(set(explicit)))
        unknown = [m for m in chosen if m not in present]
        reason = None if not unknown else (
            "explicit cohort names stream(s) with no accepted reading in the window: "
            + ", ".join(f"{m.source_id}@{m.sequence_epoch}" for m in unknown))
        mine = tuple(r for r in readings if r.member in set(chosen))
        others = tuple(m for m in present if m not in set(chosen))
        clash = _clashing(mine)
        if clash:
            return CohortSelection("AMBIGUOUS", (), alternatives=tuple(sorted(clash)),
                                   reason="the explicit cohort names overlapping epochs of one source")
        return CohortSelection("SELECTED_EXPLICIT", chosen, alternatives=others,
                               limits=series_limits(mine), reason=reason)
    if not present:
        return CohortSelection("EMPTY", (), reason="no accepted reading in the window")
    # Streams seen only in the bracket are not candidates and are not named: naming them would
    # change this window's basis whenever a neighbour day receives evidence.
    readings = tuple(r for r in readings if r.member in set(present))
    clashing = _clashing(readings)
    if clashing:
        names = sorted(clashing)
        return CohortSelection(
            "AMBIGUOUS", (), alternatives=tuple(names),
            reason=("a source reports the same time span under more than one sequence epoch ("
                    + ", ".join(f"{m.source_id}@{m.sequence_epoch}" for m in names)
                    + "); no coherent default cohort; select one explicitly"))
    successive = _multi_epoch(present)
    if successive:
        names = sorted(successive)
        return CohortSelection(
            "AMBIGUOUS", (), alternatives=tuple(names),
            reason=("a source reports in this window under more than one sequence epoch ("
                    + ", ".join(f"{m.source_id}@{m.sequence_epoch}" for m in names)
                    + "); central evidence does not attest that they are one physical stream, so the "
                      "default cohort does not blend them; select one explicitly"))
    return CohortSelection("SELECTED_UNIQUE", tuple(present), limits=series_limits(readings))


def _multi_epoch(members: list[CohortMember]) -> set[CohortMember]:
    """Members of a source (same gateway and source) present under two or more epochs."""
    by_source: dict[tuple, list[CohortMember]] = defaultdict(list)
    for m in members:
        by_source[(m.gateway_id, m.source_id)].append(m)
    return {m for group in by_source.values() if len(group) > 1 for m in group}


def _clashing(readings: tuple[AcceptedReading, ...]) -> set[CohortMember]:
    """Members of one source (same gateway and source) whose source-time spans overlap."""
    spans: dict[CohortMember, tuple[datetime, datetime]] = {}
    for r in readings:
        lo, hi = spans.get(r.member, (r.placed_at, r.placed_at))
        spans[r.member] = (min(lo, r.placed_at), max(hi, r.placed_at))
    by_source: dict[tuple, list[CohortMember]] = defaultdict(list)
    for m in sorted(spans):
        by_source[(m.gateway_id, m.source_id)].append(m)
    clashing: set[CohortMember] = set()
    for members in by_source.values():
        for i, a in enumerate(members):
            for b in members[i + 1:]:
                if _overlaps(spans[a], spans[b]):
                    clashing.update((a, b))
    return clashing


def series_limits(readings: tuple[AcceptedReading, ...]) -> tuple[CohortLimit, ...]:
    """Spans where a member's channel is not one public series (see module docstring)."""
    channels: dict[tuple, list[AcceptedReading]] = defaultdict(list)
    for r in readings:
        channels[(r.member, r.role, str(r.asset_id))].append(r)
    limits: list[CohortLimit] = []
    for (member, role, asset), rows in sorted(channels.items(), key=lambda kv: str(kv[0])):
        at_time: dict[datetime, list[AcceptedReading]] = defaultdict(list)
        for r in rows:
            at_time[r.placed_at].append(r)
        for when, same in sorted(at_time.items()):
            if len(same) > 1:
                limits.append(CohortLimit("REPORTS_OVERLAP", role, asset, member, when, when,
                                          tuple(sorted(str(x.evidence_id) for x in same))))
        ordered = sorted(rows, key=lambda r: r.sequence)
        latest: AcceptedReading | None = None
        for r in ordered:
            if latest is not None and r.placed_at < latest.placed_at:
                limits.append(CohortLimit("SEQUENCE_ORDER_INVERTED", role, asset, member, r.placed_at,
                                          latest.placed_at,
                                          tuple(sorted((str(r.evidence_id), str(latest.evidence_id))))))
            if latest is None or r.placed_at > latest.placed_at:
                latest = r
    return tuple(limits)
def step(text: str) -> None:
    s4.step(text)


def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog="tools/s402c_late_power_walkthrough.py")
    parser.add_argument("command", choices=("run", "drop"))
    parser.add_argument("--work", default=str(DEFAULT_WORK))
    parser.add_argument("--artifact", default=None)
    parser.add_argument("--tls-port", type=int, default=8918)
    parser.add_argument("--http-port", type=int, default=8919)
    parser.add_argument("--cadence-seconds", type=int, default=60)
    parser.add_argument("--db", default=DB, help="own database (dropped and recreated by run)")
    args = parser.parse_args(argv)
    if not args.db.startswith("assetops_s402c3walk") or not args.db.replace("_", "").isalnum():
        raise SystemExit(f"Refusing database {args.db!r}: use an assetops_s402c3walk* database of its own.")
    s2.DEMO_DB = args.db
    if args.command == "drop":
        import psycopg

        admin = s2.PG_ADMIN.format(pw=os.environ.get("ASSETOPS_PG_PASSWORD", "assetops-local-dev"),
                                   port=os.environ.get("ASSETOPS_PG_PORT", "55432"))
        with psycopg.connect(admin, autocommit=True) as c:
            c.execute(f"DROP DATABASE IF EXISTS {args.db} WITH (FORCE)")
        print(f"dropped {args.db}")
        work = Path(args.work)
        if work.exists() and work.name.startswith("s402c3"):
            shutil.rmtree(work)
            print(f"removed {work}")
        return 0
    walk = LateWalk(args)
    walk.args_work = args.work
    walk.run()
    return 1 if walk.failures else 0

    def windows_over(self, lo: datetime, hi: datetime) -> list[WindowBasis]:
        return [w for w in self.usable if w.start <= hi and lo < w.end]


def _segments_in(intervals: list[IntervalBasis], lo: datetime, hi: datetime) -> list[tuple[Seg, IntervalBasis]]:
    return [(s, i) for i in intervals for s in i.segments if s.start < hi and lo < s.end]


def _stop_proof(site: _Site, asset: str, lo: datetime, hi: datetime, off_a: datetime,
                off_b: datetime) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Q17: the selected runs' accepted generator reports confirm the stop (lo = last ON, hi = next first ON):
    both OFF endpoints are present, and every report strictly between lo and hi is an accepted GOOD in-cohort
    OFF, in CURRENT windows that are not Indeterminate and carry no generator series limit over the stop."""
    def refused(reason: str, ids: Any = ()) -> dict[str, Any]:
        return {"status": "REFUSED", "reason": reason, "evidence_ids": sorted(ids)}

    windows = [w for w in site.windows if w.start <= hi and lo < w.end]
    if not windows or not site.covered(lo, hi) or any(w not in site.usable for w in windows):
        return refused("STOP_NOT_ASSESSED")
    checked: list[StateReport] = []
    for w in windows:
        if w.reports is None:
            return refused("NO_TYPED_GENERATOR_REPORTS")
        if w.window_claim == INDETERMINATE:
            return refused("WINDOW_INDETERMINATE")
        if any(a <= hi and lo <= b for a, b in w.generator_limits):
            return refused("GENERATOR_SERIES_AMBIGUOUS")
        mine = [r for r in w.reports if r.asset == asset and w.start <= r.at < w.end and lo < r.at < hi]
        for reason, bad in (("UNACCEPTED_REPORT", [r for r in mine if r.disposition != "ACCEPTED"]),
                            ("NOT_GOOD_REPORT", [r for r in mine if not r.clean]),
                            ("WRONG_COHORT_REPORT", [r for r in mine if r.member not in w.members]),
                            ("CONTRARY_REPORT", [r for r in mine if not r.off])):
            if bad:
                return refused(reason, (r.evidence_id for r in bad))
        checked += mine
    first = [r.evidence_id for r in checked if off_a in (r.at, r.interval_start)]
    last = [r.evidence_id for r in checked if off_b in (r.at, r.interval_start)]
    if not first or not last:
        return refused("OFF_ENDPOINT_NOT_IN_SELECTED_RUN")
    return {"status": "CONFIRMED", "off_after_evidence_ids": sorted(first), "off_before_evidence_ids": sorted(last),
            "checked_evidence_ids": sorted(r.evidence_id for r in checked),
            "run_ids": sorted({str(w.run_id) for w in windows}),
            "window_revision_ids": sorted(str(w.window_revision_id) for w in windows),
            "reports_sha256": sha256_json(sorted(r.fact() for r in checked))}


def _stop_records(site: _Site, asset: str) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
    """Stops between consecutive runs: last ON, the first OFF after it, the last OFF before the next run, then
    its first ON, in that order, each with its report proof (Q17). A contrary ON report between the OFFs is a
    run of its own; any other contrary, non-GOOD or out-of-cohort report refuses the proof."""
    if asset not in site.stop_cache:
        runs = sorted(site.intervals(asset), key=lambda i: i.first_on)
        site.stop_cache[asset] = [
            {"start": x.last_on, "end": y.first_on, "off_after": x.off_after, "off_before": y.off_before,
             "ended_assessment_revision_id": x.revision_id, "next_assessment_revision_id": y.revision_id,
             "proof": _stop_proof(site, asset, x.last_on, y.first_on, x.off_after, y.off_before)}
            for x, y in zip(runs, runs[1:])
            if x.last_on is not None and x.off_after is not None and y.off_before is not None
            and x.last_on <= x.off_after <= y.off_before <= y.first_on]
    return site.stop_cache[asset]
    for asset in sorted({i.asset for w in site.usable for i in w.intervals}):
        spans = sorted(((s, i) for i in site.intervals(asset) for s in i.segments if s.classification == CANDIDATE),
                       key=lambda x: (x[0].start, x[0].end))
        current: _Cluster | None = None
        for span in spans:
            if current is not None and _bridges(site, asset, max(current.spans, key=lambda x: x[0].end), span):
                current.spans.append(span)
                continue
            current = _Cluster(asset, [span])
            out.append(current)
    return out


def _overlaps(c: _Cluster, e: Episode) -> bool:
    return c.asset == e.asset and c.start <= e.episode_end and e.episode_start <= c.end


def _support_write(site: _Site, finding_id: str, cluster: _Cluster, transition: str, *,
                   anchor: datetime | None, extra: dict[str, Any]) -> Write:
    intervals = {i.revision_id: i for _, i in cluster.spans}
    start, end = cluster.start, cluster.end
    anchored = next((i for s, i in cluster.spans if anchor is not None and s.start <= anchor <= s.end), None)
    primary = anchored or cluster.spans[0][1]
    windows = {site.window_of[r].window_revision_id: site.window_of[r] for r in intervals}
    segs = _segments_in(list(intervals.values()), start, end) or [(s, i) for s, i in cluster.spans]
    minutes = {c: sum((_minutes(max(s.start, start), min(s.end, end)) for s, _ in segs if s.classification == c),
                      Fraction(0)) for c in (CANDIDATE, NECESSARY, INDETERMINATE)}
    minutes[CANDIDATE] = sum((_minutes(s.start, s.end) for s, _ in cluster.spans), Fraction(0))
    cited = sorted({e for s, _ in cluster.spans for e in s.evidence_ids})
    limits = sorted({x for i in intervals.values() for x in i.limitations})
    if anchor is not None and anchored is None:
        limits.append("ANCHOR_OUTSIDE_SUPPORT: the episode's anchor is no longer inside a Candidate segment")
    claim = {
        "schema_version": 1,
        "statement": "Bounded candidate avoidable generator running: only the Candidate segments are counted; "
                     "Necessary and Indeterminate portions stay separate and the whole interval is not avoidable.",
        "candidate_spans": [{"start": iso(s.start), "end": iso(s.end), "minutes": _num(_minutes(s.start, s.end)),
                             "assessment_revision_id": i.revision_id, "evidence_ids": list(s.evidence_ids)}
                            for s, i in cluster.spans],
        "other_portions": [{"start": iso(max(s.start, start)), "end": iso(min(s.end, end)),
                            "classification": s.classification, "observed": s.observed,
                            "assessment_revision_id": i.revision_id}
                           for s, i in segs if s.classification != CANDIDATE],
        "continuity": _continuity(site, cluster),
        **extra,
    }
    return _write(site, finding_id, transition, SUPPORTED, CANDIDATE, primary, list(intervals.values()),
                  list(windows.values()), start, end, minutes, claim, limits, cited)


def _write(site: _Site, finding_id: str, transition: str, lifecycle: str, claim_level: str,
           primary: IntervalBasis | WindowBasis, intervals: list[IntervalBasis], windows: list[WindowBasis],
           start: datetime, end: datetime, minutes: dict[str, Fraction], claim: dict[str, Any],
           limits: list[str], cited: list[str]) -> Write:
    if isinstance(primary, IntervalBasis):
        primary_id, primary_run = primary.revision_id, primary.run_id
        window_rev = site.window_of[primary.revision_id].window_revision_id
    else:
        primary_id, primary_run, window_rev = primary.window_revision_id, primary.run_id, primary.window_revision_id
    assessments = [(i.revision_id, i.run_id, "INTERVAL", i.revision_id == primary_id) for i in intervals]
    assessments += [(w.window_revision_id, w.run_id, "WINDOW", w.window_revision_id == primary_id)
                    for w in windows if w.window_revision_id]
    assessments = sorted(set(assessments))
    inputs = sorted({w.input_sha256 for w in windows if w.input_sha256})
    # The supported facts, which SUPPORT_CHANGED means; provenance (revision IDs, inputs) is not in it.
    support = sha256_json({"lifecycle": lifecycle, "bounds": [iso(start), iso(end)],
                           "minutes": {k: _num(v) for k, v in minutes.items()}, "limitations": sorted(limits),
                           "spans": [[x["start"], x["end"], x["minutes"]] for x in claim.get("candidate_spans", [])],
                           "relations": _relation_facts(claim)})
    # The basis is the supported facts plus every selected interval and window revision, the window input
    # digests (controls, bindings, evidence) and the cited evidence: a changed selected window revision
    # appends a revision even with equal minutes (D-2026-10-12-s4-finding-phase-1-clarifications).
    basis = sha256_json({"matcher": MATCHER_VERSION, "model": site.model, "support": support,
                         "intervals": sorted(i.revision_id for i in intervals),
                         "windows": sorted(w.window_revision_id for w in windows if w.window_revision_id),
                         "inputs": inputs, "cited": cited,
                         # Q20: the full relation records (proof evidence IDs, report digests, assessment refs).
                         "relations": sha256_json({"continuity": claim.get("continuity") or [],
                                                   "recurrence": claim.get("recurrence")})})
    return Write(finding_id, transition, lifecycle, claim_level, site.model, primary_id, primary_run, window_rev,
                 start, end, minutes, claim, limits, assessments, cited, inputs, basis, support)


def _relation_facts(claim: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """The supported relation facts (Q20): gap bounds, what bridged them, stop OFF bounds and proof status, and
"""Typed seam between the accepted-evidence ledger and a pure analytic (S3-01 phase 2).

`EvidenceQuery` names what is asked (tenant/Site/domain, UTC half-open window,
knowledge cutoff, optional explicit cohort). `EvidenceQueryResult` is what the
repository returns: accepted public readings only (never rejected, duplicate
or conflicting deliveries), each with its exact evidence ID, envelope digest,
gateway/source/epoch/sequence/batch provenance, as-accepted Foundation and
binding revision IDs, and the server times kept apart (source, publication,
receipt, acceptance). It also returns the public configuration selected at the
cutoff: time-valid Foundation controls and the role bindings of the selected
binding sets.

`AnalyticInputSet` is the frozen input of one analytic run: the selected public
cohort's readings, the configuration, role coverage, window and model version,
with a canonical SHA-256 over all of them. Nothing in this module reads a
database, an envelope's raw JSON, a Lab run or a file-backed assessment; the
pure analytic receives this object and nothing else (D-2026-10-11-s3-dispatch-
interval-assessment, "Input and cohort").
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import hashlib
import json
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from datetime import datetime, timedelta, timezone
from typing import Any
from uuid import UUID

#: Bracketing evidence read on each side of a window, so an interval that
#: starts inside it can be bounded and continued (dispatch-3 table, "Windows").
BRACKET = timedelta(hours=6)


def utc(instant: datetime) -> datetime:
    if instant.tzinfo is None:
        raise ValueError("timestamps must be timezone-aware UTC")
    return instant.astimezone(timezone.utc)


def iso(instant: datetime | None) -> str | None:
    return None if instant is None else utc(instant).strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%S.%fZ")


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Window:
    """UTC half-open `[start, end)`."""

    start: datetime
    end: datetime

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        object.__setattr__(self, "start", utc(self.start))
        object.__setattr__(self, "end", utc(self.end))
        if not self.start < self.end:
            raise ValueError("a window needs start < end")

    def contains(self, instant: datetime) -> bool:
        return self.start <= instant < self.end

    def widened(self, by: timedelta) -> "Window":
        return Window(self.start - by, self.end + by)

    def payload(self) -> dict[str, str | None]:
        return {"start": iso(self.start), "end": iso(self.end)}


@dataclass(frozen=True, order=True)
class CohortMember:
    """One public report stream: a registered source on one sequence epoch, via one gateway."""

    gateway_id: UUID
    source_id: UUID
    sequence_epoch: str

    def payload(self) -> dict[str, str]:
        return {"gateway_id": str(self.gateway_id), "source_id": str(self.source_id),
                "sequence_epoch": self.sequence_epoch}


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class EvidenceQuery:
    tenant_id: UUID
    site_id: UUID
    domain: str
    window: Window
    knowledge_at: datetime
    #: None = default selection (a unique coherent cohort, else ambiguity).
    cohort: tuple[CohortMember, ...] | None = None
    #: Canonical roles the domain reads (empty = every role).
    roles: tuple[str, ...] = ()
    bracket: timedelta = BRACKET

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        object.__setattr__(self, "knowledge_at", utc(self.knowledge_at))
        if self.cohort is not None:
            object.__setattr__(self, "cohort", tuple(sorted(set(self.cohort))))

    @property
    def read_range(self) -> Window:
        return self.window.widened(self.bracket)


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class AcceptedReading:
        AND (status = 'COMPLETED') = (completed_at IS NOT NULL)
    )
);

        return self._snapshot(work)

    def accepted_evidence(self, tenant_id: UUID, site_id: UUID, message_ids: list[str]) -> dict[str, str]:
        """Accepted Evidence ID of each cited message ID that is accepted centrally in this Site."""
        if not message_ids:
            return {}
        return self._snapshot(lambda c: {r[0]: str(r[1]) for r in c.execute(text(
            "SELECT e.message_id, e.id FROM accepted_evidence e JOIN ingestion_disposition d "
            "ON d.accepted_evidence_id = e.id AND d.decision = 'ACCEPTED' WHERE e.tenant_id = :t AND e.site_id = :s "
            "AND e.message_id = ANY(:m)"), {"t": tenant_id, "s": site_id, "m": list(message_ids)}).all()})

    def selected_intervals(self, tenant_id: UUID, site_id: UUID, analytic_id: str, domain: str,
                           bracket: timedelta) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
        """INTERVAL revisions of each window's last DEFAULT selection, with that run's window limitations
        and every unfinished or failed recompute job overlapping the window's read range (the read's
        freshness rule: a matching prior result stays stale while a job is outstanding)."""
        def work(c: Connection) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
            heads = c.execute(text(
                "SELECT DISTINCT ON (window_start, window_end) run_id, window_start, window_end "
                "FROM analytic_run_selection WHERE tenant_id = :t AND site_id = :s AND analytic_id = :a "
                "ORDER BY window_start, window_end, seq DESC"), {"t": tenant_id, "s": site_id, "a": analytic_id})
            out = []
            for run_id, ws, we in heads.all():
                jobs = [{"id": str(j[0]), "status": j[1]} for j in c.execute(text(
                    "SELECT id, status FROM recompute_job WHERE tenant_id = :t AND site_id = :s AND domain = :d "
                    f"AND {_LIVE_JOBS} AND window_start < :hi AND window_end >= :lo "
                    "ORDER BY created_at, id"), {"t": tenant_id, "s": site_id, "d": domain, "a": analytic_id, "lo": ws - bracket,
                                                 "hi": we + bracket}).all()]
                revisions = [_revision_row(r) for r in c.execute(text(
                    f"SELECT {_REVISION_COLUMNS}, l.scope FROM analytic_run_assessment l JOIN assessment_revision v "
                    "ON v.id = l.assessment_revision_id WHERE l.run_id = :r"), {"r": run_id}).all()]
                limits = [x for r in revisions if r["scope"] == "WINDOW"
                          for x in (r["result"] or {}).get("limitations", [])]
                out += [{**r, "window_limitations": limits, "window_jobs": jobs}
                        for r in revisions if r["scope"] == "INTERVAL"]
            return out

        return self._snapshot(work)
